ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U1	Hi! Power! Question Time. Requesting presence check! 38 members of the Riigikogu are present, 63 are absent. Minister of Defence Sven Mikser participates in Question Time in the duties of Prime Minister, Minister of Finance Maris Lauri and Minister of Agriculture Ivari Padar. Four members of the Riigikogu have registered as a questioner and it makes sense to submit two additional questions.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U2	The first questioner is Kadri Simson, and the question is to the Minister of Defence in charge of the Prime Minister Sven Mikser. Please, Kadri Simson!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U3	Thank you. Dear Sven Mikser! My question concerns the tax system. As we know, most European Union countries have progressive income tax, the only exceptions are Estonia, Latvia, Lithuania, Bulgaria, Romania and Hungary. In Estonia, only the Centre Party has transferred the draft progressive income tax to the Riigikogu. We've started it several times. The Social Democrats haven't done that so far, but yesterday you introduced your plan. I would like to know more about how you see progressive income tax. Do you see this as an opportunity to collect a lower tax on people with lower incomes than before or vice versa, and you think that a higher level of income should be allocated to the state budget?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U4	Thank you, Honourable Asker. As a Social Democrat, I naturally support progressive taxation. However, this is a much broader concept than the mere graduation of personal income tax. Once again, as a social democrat, I do not view the objective of taxation very narrowly, i.e. either as a means of creating greater social justice and social security or as a means of obtaining revenue for the state budget. I think that the revenue side of the budget is extremely important for the functioning of one country. Nearly all countries around the world use taxation as a source of fiscal revenue, taxes on income, taxes on consumption, taxes on assets, taxes on vices. In addition to earning budget revenue, taxes also have other objectives, such as directing people to sustainably consume, not harming their health through the use of drugs, etc. Of course, we should not ignore the principle that, apart from the tax to be paid, account should also be taken of the amount of income that a person may have in his or her ability to pay taxes. As a Social Democrat, I also attach great importance to this principle. It is certainly important, on the one hand, to ease the tax burden on people who, because of their low income, have to apply for social benefits to support themselves and their families. On the other hand, it is reasonable to ask for a somewhat higher tax on those who earn significantly higher than the average.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U5	Kadri Simson, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U6	Thank you. Dear Sven Mikser! I fully agree with you that, at the moment, the tax burden on people is unequally distributed and that it is too high for people with lower incomes. But when you come back to progressive income tax, you have proposed that the share of income of a natural person exceeding EUR 1600 per month be taxed at 29%, i.e. at a higher tax rate. Please explain whether the higher rate is set for gross income exceeding EUR 1600 or taxable income.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U7	Thank you. Just as I know you have offered, the progressive tax models of social democrats are based on the principle that the share of income that exceeds a certain amount in one calendar month is taxed at the next level of taxation. By the way, in an interesting detail, the IRL has also included in its graduated income tax model a different taxation of income components, such as the share of income from zero to 500 euros, but the next share of income, up to 846 euros, is taxed at an unusually high rate of 40%. We have foreseen that in one calendar month, an amount of income exceeding EUR 1600 will, of course, be paid on an annual basis at a rate of 29%. It is certainly also justified to review this amount year after year and to adjust it to the increase in average wages as well as the cost of living.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U8	Kadri Simson, another question, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U9	Thank you. In your first reply, you mentioned that people are also able to win by other taxes, and I noticed that you were no longer talking about VAT in the tax programme introduced yesterday. If, in the past, there have been proposals for a regressive reduction of VAT in your election programme, then there have now been no proposals. You have no longer talked about reducing VAT on previously authorised food products, medicines or cultural events. Have the Social Democrats, while in government, abandoned the reduction in VAT rates for such important groups of goods?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U10	Certainly, when drawing up the tax package, the tax programme must be very closely monitored in order to ensure that the promised is also responsible. The Socialist Tax Package is. Of course, different times require different solutions. A lot of attention has been drawn to the fact that labour taxes should be reduced in Estonia. The Social Democrats have taken this problem to the fullest extent and we have offered a number of social tax differentiations in our tax programme. On the one hand, for those whose competitiveness on the labour market is less than normal and for whom health insurance is guaranteed without an employment relationship, we foresee an exemption from the social tax from the health insurance component. On the other hand, since the employment register has now been successfully implemented, we suggest that if you work part-time, you could opt out of the so-called "minimum rate" for the pension insurance part of the social tax and collect the pension insurance tax according to the person's actual workload. In addition, we have also considered the specificity of regional social tax. These measures fulfil a number of very good objectives. Firstly, it encourages part-time work, because there are people for whom employers may not be looking very well at finding work in the first place if they are not able to work full-time. Secondly, it will help people with less competitiveness to enter the labour market. Third, it allows jobs to develop and stay outside larger centres, outside Tallinn and Harjumaa. So, of course, tax distinctions, tax reductions at different rates, serve a number of purposes. This is precisely what we consider to be extremely important at this time in terms of employment and job creation.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U11	Thank you, Minister. Heimar Lenk, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U12	Thank you, esteemed performer. The progressive income tax is very important to us, and I am thinking above all of our country, purely based on the principles of social justice. Of course, disputes about taxes are equally important. However, it seems to me that instead of changing the tax system, we should talk more about bringing revenue to the Estonian state. In my opinion, EUR 8.4 billion for our incomes is not a very large amount. Figuratively speaking, the Republic of Estonia should earn more, then there would be more to share. How do you think we're gonna do that?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U13	Thank you. Of course, making a profit is extremely important. I would point out that the success of countries today is not only measured by growth indicators, although it is also an important component. Economic growth is known to be often cyclical and a very rapid growth phase may be followed by a period of decline or long-term stagnation. If we measure the success of countries, the UN Human Development Index is one of the good indicators that, apart from economic growth, takes into account a number of other indicators, such as life expectancy, healthy life years, education, access to education, both formally and informally. In this index, Estonia became the world's top thirty even before the big crisis, in the boom years. Unfortunately, in times of crisis, but also in times of recovery from the crisis, we are in a state of stagnation. I think that the key now is to focus on those areas where we are far behind the European average, but also from the developed countries of the world. These are real quality of life indicators. There is certainly a need to pay attention to the creation of jobs in Estonia, especially'smart jobs', and to be able to invest in them. Jobs that generate high added value should be created so that the resulting services-goods can be exported and sold expensively. Then there will also be prosperity for the people of Estonia. So I agree that earning income is important and that the country's success is determined by a number of factors. We may seem very successful in certain areas, but at the same time we have areas that hinder development, which should not be overlooked in addition to enjoying overall success.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U14	Viktor Vassilyev, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U15	Thank you, Mr President of the Riigikogu. Good Minister of Defence in the role of Prime Minister! In most countries of the world, the country's tax revenue largely consists of direct taxes, i.e. taxes on assets and taxes on assets, with a smaller proportion of indirect taxes, i.e. excise duties and VAT. In Estonia, on the contrary, 60% of the state's tax revenue is indirect taxes, i.e. excise duties and VAT, and a smaller proportion is income tax. This means that since the poor are always considerably more than the rich, it is probably the law of nature, the poorer part of the Estonian population makes a greater contribution to the state's livelihood. Please explain to us why it is good for the people of Estonia that it is the poorer people who are making a greater contribution to sustaining their country.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U16	Thank you. I certainly don't think that's good. If we look at the so-called tax wedge, we can see that a person earning average income in Estonia has been taxed relatively similarly to the 'European average'. At the same time, for example, someone earning two thirds of the average income has been taxed somewhat above the European average. Yes, it confirms your statement that we pay relatively, let's say even unusually high taxes, precisely those people who make a small profit if we count all taxes together. It is clear that, for example, VAT with little distinctions means that people earning low incomes spend most of their income on purchasing essential goods and services, which are taxed at the full rate of VAT. Therefore, they contribute much more to their income through VAT than those who earn more and do not spend much of their income in Estonia for the purchase of goods and services subject to VAT. However, if we take away and analyse the issue raised by your colleague, Kadri Simson, from the lower rate of VAT on food, we will see that the winnings that we receive are part of the public revenues that people are given back or left in their hands, too, reach the pockets of those whose income is higher than the average. So in this option, it is worth analysing very seriously the feasibility of such an advantage before it is introduced. There are certainly ways to ease the relative tax burden on people with lower incomes. I think that general progressive taxation is one of the ways to do it in the most sensible and fair way.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U17	The number of additional questions allowed has been used. I will conclude on this issue.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U18	The next questioner is Sven Sester and the question is for Finance Minister Maris Lauri. Please, Sven Sester!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U19	Thank you, Mr President. Good Minister of Finance! The budget has now been adopted, but we must still look to the future, also in monetary policy. My question relates to the balance between our state budget and all our finances. Indeed, when we criticised this issue during the budgetary procedure and said that the budget was nominally negative, we were reassured by the information that there was at least a structural balance and even a structural positive surplus. It later turned out that the European Commission gave an indication that we were actually in a negative position. Then we began to be reassured by the fact that at least Eesti Pank estimates that everything is okay. On the basis of information from last week, it is now clear that Eesti Pank has spoken in its press release about the structural deficit and the resulting need for consolidation, which may require us to raise taxes or limit costs. Thus, Eesti Pank also considers that the situation is not so rosy. How do you comment on that?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U20	Thanks to the questioner! The assessments of the Estonian budget balance by the Ministry of Finance are in line with what some international institutions have done, including the IMF, OECD, as well as Eesti Pank. Therefore, the Commission's view that our budget balance is poor and weak is, as we know, that they value our economic cycle differently. I don't know how the questioner thinks, but I think that the unemployment rate of 10 to 11% is normal in Estonia's conditions. This is based on the Commission's assessment of the budgetary balance. It is strange to think that our unemployment could be much lower. Therefore, I trust more about the IMF, the OECD, the Ministry of Finance and Eesti Pank's assessment. I would remind you that Eesti Pank's budget balance assessments are nevertheless supported by the Ministry of Finance. The fact that Eesti Pank pointed out problems that may arise in changed economic circumstances is natural. We too see that the budget can suffer if economic conditions turn out to be worse than what we see ahead. At the same time, I would remind you that the Ministry of Finance's economic forecast is quite conservative and that all the facts have been taken into account in the risk scenario, too, that things can get worse.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U21	Sven Sester, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U22	Thank you, Mr President. Madam Secretary! Now we are back on the ground that you say, let us trust the Bank of Estonia. I also trust Eesti Pank. But they have said that we are already in a situation where we have a potential deficit. That's last week's information. I wonder why we need someone's third assessment, in this case, the European Commission's assessment, if we rely solely on the opinion of our national experts. Why is there even a need for six-pack and two-pack monitoring and independent assessment in Europe? In my opinion, we have agreed, according to the latest European rules of play, that we will follow the opinion of an independent expert and this will provide an opportunity to look much more conservatively at the countries that have faced the problems. Are you or are you no longer based on Europe? Would you be so kind as to comment?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U23	Thanks for asking. The structural balance of the budget is known to be based on an assessment of the cyclicality of GDP, i.e. what normal, or potential, growth is. Potential growth is not a specific size that can be measured. This will be assessed through different models. There is no unambiguously accepted model for assessing it. No one, no matter how big or highly valued economists, has a single understanding of what the most appropriate method is. Each method has its pros and cons, weaknesses and strengths. In this case, I would also argue that several Estonian people, as well as Eesti Pank, IMF and OECD, point to the fact that the European Commission's assessment or method, which it uses in the same way for all, is not the most correct in Estonia's circumstances. We take into account that they see things differently. We have also always informed the Commission and pointed to methodological uncertainties that must be taken into account. We hope that at some point these methods will be considered and that problems will be reviewed. We have also proposed that the Commission publish its methodology in the future so that we can take the same methodology and calculations into account when drawing up our budget. We are now using the method that we find to be the most reliable, useful and correct, but the Commission is using a different one. If we were to use the Commission's method, we would be able to draw up a more precise, correct budget. I hope that the Commission will take account of our proposal.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U24	Sven Sester, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U25	Thank you, Mr President. Madam Secretary! Do not use what is more useful to you, but use what is more objective. I think it's going to do a lot better in life. I have one more question for you, it concerns potential economic growth and it is directly linked to monetary policy, because it gives us a future indication. Indeed, by looking at the forecasts for growth for the next year, we will see that the later the forecast, the more pessimistic it is. This trend has been shown by many forecasters in recent years, including the Ministry of Finance. It can now be seen that Eesti Pank's latest forecast has fallen again, and this is even lower than the one-time forecast of the Ministry of Finance on the basis of which the budget was put together at the time. That's why I'm asking you. At what moments will the head of the finance minister start to burn, and he says, "Wait a minute, let's go over the costs." I'm not saying that this should be the case right now, but I really am interested in your opinion as Minister of Finance. When do we say that the growth forecast or the reality is already such that we should start with some kind of cost-cutting programme? Please, please, give me an overview of this as well.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U26	First of all, as a remark, we certainly do not choose what is more favourable to us, but what we think is right. Coming to your question, I say that economic forecasts are forecasts. The word 'forecast' means conjecture. Assumptions about the future can never be accurate and it is natural that they pass. If we look at projections in the long term, there are times when forecasts are constantly below expectations and there are periods where expectations are systematically exceeded. Right now, we're in a situation where everyone's hoping things will get better. Unfortunately, this has not always been the case. We don't know when it's gonna be the moment when things are going to turn out as predicted or better. In order to assess this, our daily work is to monitor various indicators and attributes, different processes, such as analysing tax receipts. Tax receipts have so far been as expected, with some tax receipts even better. This shows that there is certainly no reason to worry now about whether or not budget revenue is full and whether there are problems with it. The Ministry of Finance makes regular economic forecasts twice a year. If necessary, additional assessments and calculations will be made if more serious problems are identified. One way is to monitor VAT receipts and I am pleased to say that they have so far been as expected and even better. So right now, I don't see why I'm dealing with cuts. But it's one place you should always watch. There are, of course, other indicators.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U27	Aivar Sõerd, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U28	Honourable Chairman! Dear Minister, Last week we adopted the state budget for next year. The new budgetary process in the European Union now requires the European Commission to evaluate the budgetary plans of the Member States. I know that the Commission's assessments of Member States' budgets, including the Estonian budget, were discussed by the Council of Finance Ministers, where you also participated. Could you describe what the finance ministers think about the budgetary plans of the Member States for next year? Did they say anything about Estonia's budget plan?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U29	Thanks for asking. As we know, the European Commission divided the countries into three groups: those where there were no problems; those where there were some doubts, i.e. 'border bounds'; those where there were problems. In addition, three countries were specifically introduced, namely France, Belgium and Italy, which are asked for further questions. It was the third group that came up with questions. They were discussed, and these countries long and wide explained what plans they had, where they were strong, in which they thought the Commission had been wrong and what they have already done. There were no questions about the first two groups, there were no disputes, and this also concerns Estonia.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U30	Viktor Vassilyev, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U31	Thank you, Mr President of the Riigikogu. Good Minister of Finance! The debt of the Estonian state sector is, as we know, rather small. At the same time, it is known that private sector debt is quite high, as far as I know, even slightly higher than GDP. You know the exact number, I don't. However, there are countries where the opposite is true of the public sector in large debt, but citizens and companies hardly have any debts. The brightest example is Greece, which we all helped. Please tell me whether, as your Minister of Finance, you think that the government whose responsibility you share will continue until March and perhaps continue to pursue this policy, that it is preferable to let the people in debt precisely in order to keep the state budget balanced at all costs, even though it cannot be committed or brought on by people.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U32	Thanks for asking. I must point out that the honourable member of the Riigikogu has very wrong data. First of all, the debt of the Estonian private sector has been constantly falling and has not increased at all; it is considerably smaller than our GDP. Secondly, it is true that there are countries where sovereign debt is higher than private debt. However, for problem countries (including Greece), the situation is such that both the private sector and the public sector are in debt. This is the greatest threat if both sectors are in trouble. Our public sector's debt is modest, which, by the way, allows Estonian companies to borrow in order to invest. Businesses always need to take out loans for investment because they never have enough own resources. The volume of one-off investments in the short term is always very large. It's very difficult to collect this amount in advance, you still have to make an investment with a loan and then go on. The fact that our level of credit in the private sector is'stable' and, as I said, very fast down in recent years, shows that we do not have any problems. If we compare our private debt with that of many other countries, it is very normal. As an economic analyst, I would even say that in our private sector the debt burden could be higher, i.e. companies in particular could invest more, as they create new jobs and new growth opportunities.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U33	I will conclude on this issue.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U34	The next questioner is Helir-Valdor Seeder and the question is for the Minister of Agriculture Ivari Padar. Please, Helir-Valdor Seeder!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U35	Thank you. Dear Minister, As of 1 January 2015, the Government Coalition has decided to tax agricultural subsidies and even loss of income benefits for natural persons, such as the NATURA area. This places natural persons in an unequal position compared to self-employed persons and companies, as self-employed persons have the possibility to deduct costs of seed, fertiliser, fuel, etc. before paying taxes, but are no longer able to do so. Please tell me why the Ministry of Agriculture proposed this. I know, in my time, some of the target groups in the sector came up, not to mention pressure from the Ministry of Finance, several times with the same idea of collecting taxes for the country, which are very small, and creating an unequal situation. For what reasons did you finally decide that?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U36	Thanks for asking. This is one of the tax changes decided upon when the coalition agreement was made in the spring of this year. So in this case, we cannot talk about the very bright initiative of the Ministry of Agriculture. But speaking of the amendment, in my understanding, the first half of this, which concerns the taxation of the single area payment as well as the taxation of a natural person, can be taken clearly and logically. It is clear that single area payment can be taken as a "income-free' income and this will bring greater clarity to the sector as a whole. However, I, too, am not satisfied with the other party to the amendment that, from 1 January, income called lost income will be taxed. I am certainly working to find a solution, even in the form of a law amendment, to remedy this situation. I admit that the current approach is not the best one.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U37	Helir-Valdor Seeder, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U38	Thank you. After all, this is not only a direct grant and a loss of income benefit, but also a grant for grazing and other benefits received by natural persons. Besides, only active farmers with animals can actually get them. So, in my opinion, this problem is wider and more unfair. Did I understand correctly from your answer, Mr Minister, that you and the Social Democrats are prepared to abolish this tax, which brings EUR 1.5 million to the state budget, not more than a year, and affects more than half of the applicants for the grant? Are you ready to create a level playing field for all farmers in case you should be freed from the yoke of the Reform Party after the elections?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U39	Thank you, dear questioner. As regards one side of the taxable income, i.e. the income of agriculture, whether it is called the OPT or other income related to agriculture, I see no reason to change the law. There is a reason for a legislative amendment, particularly in the area of loss of income. For example, in the case of environmental subsidies, it is clear that this is not so-called philosophically correct.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U40	Helir-Valdor Seeder, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U41	Thank you. The last question I have is about the sugar fine. As we know, the Estonian state has long debated with the European Commission about the fine and the sugar fine for other stocks. With regard to other stocks, we finally achieved the right and this money has been repaid to the Estonian state, but it represented a smaller part of the fine. The sugar fine was much higher and its disputes remained with the previous government. The previous cabinet decided that if the Commission did not have a clear positive response to our request for a refund of the sugar fine, which is to a large extent similar to that of other stocks, the court would be seised. Can you tell me how far this government has this business?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U42	Kalev Eagle, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U43	Thank you. Let me put it this way: more or less at the stage of the dispute, as it was when you left the government, it is now. We have not received a clear position from the Commission and we will continue to follow the same logic. If we find out how the sugar fine is treated, we'll take the next steps.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U44	Thank you, Mr. Chairman. Austet Minister of Agriculture! Estonia received additional support from the European Union for milk producers due to problems in the eastern market. Is it now clear on what basis this aid is awarded, by litre or by number of animals? Is there a difference between larger and smaller producers?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U45	Thank you. The answer is, today we're going to make this information public. I can talk right here about how we foresee an additional allocation of nearly EUR 7 million. The distribution is based on the quota milk of August, September and October, on which we count it per kilogram. We are applying a differentiated approach to smaller milk producers, for whom we count the quota-based milk premium of 4.56 euro cents per kilogram. For anyone who goes more than 200000 kilograms over a three-month period, the rate of aid shall be 4,0 cents per kilo of milk. News about this is coming out today, the decision has been made. The payment is scheduled for January-February. It's not because of the election, it's just the logic that we can't pay it sooner. After that, we will, of course, continue to negotiate with the European Commission on all the crisis measures relating to milk production.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U46	Erki Arrow, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U47	Thank you, Mr President of the Riigikogu. Dear Minister, I will continue on the same issue of grants. Looking at the changes in the last few days, the changes in the financial market towards our eastern neighbour, we can see that we still have very large quantities going from us to Russia, albeit not directly, but through Lithuanian purchasers. How do you see, in the current market situation, that if we receive EUR 7 million of support, is enough? It must also be borne in mind that spring sowing is coming soon, although cheaper fuel is now slightly compensating for the situation of farmers. Is the €7 million grant sufficient or not for now?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U48	Thank you. As far as fuel is concerned, of course, we are in a golden age, there is nothing to complain about. However, as I said, EUR 7 million will compensate for part of the loss of income in 2014. Negotiations will certainly continue with the European Commission to implement emergency measures. On the other hand, I say that raising the aid in particular in the dairy sector looks sometimes like wearing a vicious water. This usually means that just as much as you increase support, prices are falling in some reprocessed industry. This is actually a bigger problem for Estonian agriculture. As far as the Russian market is concerned, of course, it is close and attractive. But even if the Russian market should open again and when it comes to deciding how much we are going back to this market, my deep recommendation to the entire agricultural sector in Estonia is this: nine times to measure and one time to cut. In any case, the current situation is positive in that it has pulled us out of the comfort position and made us look for new markets. That's what we do.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U49	I will conclude on this issue.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U50	The next questioner is Margus Tsahkna and the question is the Prime Minister's role for the Minister of Defence. Please, Margus Tsahkna!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U51	Thank you. I ask my question at the request of disabled people's organisations and this relates to an article published in the Eesti Päevaleht, entitled 'The organisations of disabled people received money from those who did not object to the reform'. The total number of people with disabilities organisations is 16 and they have received equal annual support. The article shows that social democrats have made changes according to which taxpayers' money from the state budget is divided into only a selected part of disability organisations. How do you justify, or according to which you have made such a choice, that it is precisely these organisations of the disabled that will receive additional state budget support and many others will not receive it?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U52	Thank you, Honourable Asker. As far as I know, grants to various organisations were entered into the state budget between second and third readings, i.e. when the Government of the Republic no longer participated in the state budget processing process. If you ask for my opinion, of course, the more the Riigikogu finds during the budgetary procedure, the more money to support the organisations that do the work that is worthwhile, the better, be the association of homeowners or associations of people with disabilities. Grants must, of course, comply with the basic budget law. In different years, the Riigikogu has indeed found money for organisations, but not only for them. It is also very worthwhile to find an opportunity to support some local government investments and objects. But being here today as Minister of Defence in charge of Prime Minister duties, I would like to step out of my role very far if I were to evaluate which amendments the Committee on Monetary Affairs of the Riigikogu or the House considers necessary to support and which amendments do not.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U53	Margus Tsahkna, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U54	Thank you. Minister, thank you very much. You are also chairman of the Social Democratic Party, and that is why I am asking these questions so that you can explain. If you look at the proposals that were made by the Sociers, this article simply reveals to you that it is precisely those organisations who later supported the reform of the capacity to work that received additional support. Those who opposed the reform and expressed the views of their members did not receive support. That is why, as a member of the Riigikogu, I ask you whether there is any connection to this subject. Unfortunately, as a member of the Riigikogu, I have also had to explain that we do not buy votes or influence organisations with state budget allocations. What is your position on this?
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U55	As far as I know, there's no such connection. I must honestly admit that I have not, of course, been very attentive to the debate on the reform of working capacity and the content of what organisations or individuals have been involved in debates or demonstrations, whether in favour or against the reform. I am fully aware that the reform has received a great deal of attention, including acute criticism from a number of organisations. But I cannot comment on the links between the state budget funding of organisations and their support or non-support.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U57	Thank you. Let me ask you a little bit more. There are 16 disability organisations, as I said, and they represent over 100,000 people. ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U58	Thank you. I think that the process of processing the Riigikogu needs full clarity, and that has been the case for years. The clearer, the better. I'm sure there's more to it. On the issue of money in the press, which is considered to be 'roof money', I say this: according to the basic law, one of the drafts of the state budget is the exclusive right of the Government of the Republic to initiate. The amendments to be made in the Riigikogu are also subject to very strict rules. Changes to increase costs must also be accompanied by a proposal to increase revenue or to reduce any other expenditure. In my view, it is absolutely inevitable that the state budget as a whole is a political document which, in the form proposed by the government, reflects to a large extent the political priorities of the government. At the same time, the 'basic mass' of expenditure has already been prescribed by law. I think that Estonia should not take as an example countries such as the one found in the world, where the role of Parliament is limited to voting for or against the budget. The Riigikogu must also have the opportunity to make changes to the budget, including on small items of expenditure that Parliament considers necessary to support, be it a sports organisation, a non-profit organisation or an investment object. All such cost lines are justified, necessary and gratifying. Of course, there are always those who do not receive as much support as they hope. And there are certainly many who do not receive as much support as they need. So, for my part, the more Riigikogu finds these possibilities, the better it will be to provide additional funding for various organisations, including the organisations of people with disabilities who are undoubtedly doing a commendable job, in the course of the budgetary process.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U83	Our work yesterday ended in a situation where the first reading of Bill 742 came to the conclusion that the rapporteur from the initiators of the draft had made a presentation, but the members of the Riigikogu could not ask him questions. I therefore ask Mikhail Stalnukhin to come back to the speaker's desk and the MPs can ask. Vladimir Velman, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U84	Thank you very much, Mr President. Since there were hardly any people in this Chamber at the end of yesterday's working day and the honourable rapporteur did not have the substance of the draft to explain to anyone, may I ask you, honourable speaker, to refer once again what the content of this draft is and what is behind it.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U85	Thank you for your question, that is very relevant. It's really nice to see so many people in the hall, and I'm happy to explain it to them. The faction of the Estonian Centre Party has introduced a draft amendment to the Act, the content of which is as follows. § 6 of the Citizenship Act lays down the conditions for obtaining Estonian citizenship. There are many and different types of residence, such as at least 15 years old, long-term residence permit or permanent residence permit, etc. These include section 3: must be able to speak Estonian in accordance with the requirements of § 8 of this Act. And point 4: must know the Constitution of the Republic of Estonia and the Citizenship Act in accordance with the requirements of § 9 of this Act. Our proposal, behind which the tradition is, I would say, over 75 years long, is that we offer to include Section 6 (2) in the law, which reads: 'A foreigner who has been born in Estonia before 20.08.1991 or has moved to Estonia is exempt from the requirements of subsection 1 (3) and (4) of this section. ' I would not say that such a proposal contains something new, and in order to prove it, we will turn to the Citizenship Act of the Republic of Estonia. I'm going to show you how some of the provisions sounded in that law in 1938. Thus, in the Citizenship Act of 1938, Section 2 "Baseds and Order of Nationalization', in which a section, § 6, stipulated that an alien who wishes to enter Estonian citizenship under the naturalization procedure must meet the following conditions. Three conditions are listed: age, length of residence in Estonia and thirdly, you must know the Estonian language. Next, Section 7 states that the requirements of subsection 6 (2) and (3) concerning two years of living in Estonia and knowledge of the Estonian language may be omitted for certain persons. There are three categories of persons in the letter: of course, persons of Estonian origin; those who have special services in front of the Republic of Estonia and thirdly, stateless persons who have resided permanently in Estonia until the date of application for admission to Estonian citizenship. I mean, the proposal of the Group of the Estonian Centre Party is nothing new. This principle existed before the war in 1938. And that doesn't mean it hasn't been followed up. For example, in the late 1980s, in the early 1990s, hopefully you will remember this, the Estonian Congress used such things as the blue card and the green card. 20500 people received the citizenship of the Republic of Estonia on the basis of a green card. Later, the Estonian Congress and its successors, the Pro Patria Union, emerged and we have the former members of this former party, which no longer exists, now in this Chamber, and this principle should be close to them. Why the Estonian Congress was based on principles that were honest in 1938 but in the 1990s, the change in citizenship policy changed sharply, of course, can be justified. It was understood that privatisation would begin, privatisation would begin and the desire to leave a very large part of the population outside this process was, of course, a very convincing reason to change the citizenship law so that its principles would not be met by those in force in 1938. Somewhere in the late 1990s, according to my memory in 1999, the privatisation agency ceased its work, but by then there were other reasons to make it difficult for non-Estonians living in Estonia to acquire the citizenship of the Republic of Estonia. I would like to remind you, for example, of the 2003 Riigikogu elections. The then Isamaa party received 7% of the votes and even less moderate, 6%. Since the respective calculations were made by scientists, many at the time knew that if the naturalisation citizens had been 30,000 more than they had been then, one party of these two would certainly not have gotten into the Riigikogu. And if there had been more of these citizens by 60,000, neither party would most likely have gotten into Parliament. Imagine what our political landscape would have been like by now! But that doesn't mean that the great idea of giving citizenship a simplified procedure is forgotten. The first party to come back to this idea was Res Publica, which does not exist at the moment, but its former members are entirely present in this Chamber. Res Publica issued a newspaper Take the Power in Russian I'm not going to call it in just and make very intelligent decisions. For example, in October 2002 (No 3), half a page was dedicated to Juhan Parts: a beautiful picture, a finger up and a slogan 'Support a new policy! '. What was this new policy about? Here is the position of Res Publica on the issue of citizenship. It is clearly stated that it is necessary to change the scheme: this scheme, that first the language, then the mind and then the citizenship, is wrong. The scheme must look like this: first citizenship, then mind and language. I agree with that. This was accepted by our ancestors in the 1930's, why should we resist? And there are also specific proposals in this paper. By the way, three people have signed this part of the page, two of whom I would certainly like to mention: one is Rein Taagepera and the other is Urmas Reinsalu. It has been formulated here that persons who have resided permanently on the territory of Estonia before August 1991, who have a residence permit and who have not been punished in criminal proceedings, should become Estonian citizens when they apply for it. Of course, they must be loyal. Civic courses have also been mentioned here, and nothing can be said about them. We now have this situation that the Estonian Central Party has come up with the idea of giving citizenship to a simplified procedure and one of the political parties IRL, which consists of the former Fatherland Association and Res Publica, who have both used this idea in practice, giving 20500 people a simplified citizenship procedure, has offered it in their programming documents and discussed it in their councils and management. The question arises as to whether the score is two: 2. In fact, the situation is not 2:2 because we still have social democrats in the hall. The Social Democrats know very well that their top politicians, such as Urve Palo, have also supported this idea. In order not to be mistaken, I'm going to read you a quote. 'I support the call for a point,' says Urve Palo, on 20 February of this year, 'which requires that non-citizens born in Estonia be granted Estonian citizenship by a simplified procedure. In fact, the state could introduce such a regime for all "grey passport" holders who have lived in Estonia before 26 February 1992, as well as for their children. Of course, they themselves must express their wish to become an Estonian citizen. ' So, ladies and gentlemen, I'm just stating the fact. This is a draft whose initiators rely on the position of both the former components of the IRL and the current Social Democratic Party. You have said so and it would be interesting to know why you do not want to support it now. But it is up to us to bring this idea to this hall and to propose that we support this very good legislative amendment. Yes, the difference is that Res Publica and the Fatherland Alliance talked about August 1991, but SDE somehow offers February 1992, but I think we can solve this problem between two readings. Thank you, colleagues.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U135	Our next item is the second reading of the draft law amending the Citizenship Act and the State Fees Act 737 initiated by the Government of the Republic. The rapporteur is Indrek Saar, member of the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U136	Ladies and gentlemen, The Government of the Republic initiated a draft of 737 this year on 6 October. The first reading took place on 12 November. The deadline for submitting amendments was 26 November and only the Central Party Group submitted the amendments within the deadline. There were three of them. The Committee on Constitutional Affairs discussed the preparation of the second reading at its meeting on 8 December. The Minister of the Interior and another official of the Ministry of the Interior also took part in the meeting. The Commission discussed and voted on the three amendments tabled by the Centre Party Group. Following the introduction of the full text of the new State Fees Act, the Commission also decided to propose two amendments itself. A little of the content of the amendments. The first amendment of the Centre Party was to supplement the draft with a new clause 9 which states that the examination of the Constitution and Citizenship Act should not be carried out by a person who has completed primary or secondary education according to the Estonian national curriculum. The Commission considered that at present the provision of social education in comprehensive school was not organised in such a way that the acquisition of these knowledge would be controlled, as is the case with a language examination with clear categories. The Commission therefore did not support this amendment. Amendment 2 was also tabled by the Central Party Group. It proposed to amend the provisions of § 1 (16) of the draft law so that § 13 (4) of the Citizenship Act is worded as follows: 'A minor under 15 years of age, born in Estonia or moving permanently to Estonia immediately after birth with his or her parent or parents, shall receive Estonian citizenship by naturalisation from the moment of birth if one of his or her parents or parents raising a child alone, who is not considered by any state to be a citizen under the laws in force, has been legally resident in Estonia for at least 5 years at the time of birth. " Both parents are mentioned in the existing text. The first half of this amendment would, according to the Centre Party's explanation, ensure that a situation where a child is deprived of his or her citizenship is excluded because he or she has only one parent officially known. This proposal was considered in the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. The other half of this proposal, which would allow naturalisation to be granted Estonian citizenship to children who are raised alone by a person who is not considered by any state to be a citizen under the laws in force, was not supported in the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. This was not supported by the initiator's representative in the form of the Minister of the Interior. The principle is that the purpose of giving Estonian citizenship to a child born in Estonia of parents of undefined nationality is to ensure that the child is able to acquire citizenship according to the country of residence, because no other country considers him or her to be its own citizen. However, in such a situation, only a child who does not acquire the citizenship of any country with the birth of a parent, i.e. a ius sanguini according to the principle. However, the first part of this amendment was supported by the Commission and was the subject of Amendment 3, which was reworded by the Committee on Constitutional Affairs. It found support. Amendment 4 is tabled by the Centre Party Group. Its content is that a person of 65 years of age applying for Estonian citizenship can take an oral examination in Russian when applying for citizenship. The Commission did not support this proposal because the prerequisite for obtaining Estonian citizenship is that a person must speak the Estonian language at the required level for everyday life. Amendments 5 and 6 are technical in nature and result from the adoption on 10 December of the full text of the State Fees Act, which will already be in force on 1 January. Consequently, the amendments that were included in the original draft are no longer necessary. The decision of the Constitutional Committee was to put the draft on the agenda of the plenary session of the Riigikogu for a second reading today and to propose that the second reading be stopped.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U148	Thank you for your question. There are simply different opinions as to whether they change or not. As regards whether the content of the application for stateless parents must be one way or the other, we can argue about this infinitely. I quote the Minister for the Interior who has taken office after you, who certainly does not agree with the statement that the naturalisation procedure is currently guaranteed by the parents writing a statement that the child will receive the citizenship of the country in which he or she was born if neither of them has nationality. In the new version, they agree that they will not write a statement that the child will be granted citizenship, but if they do not wish to have citizenship at the place of birth, they will write a statement and go through the relevant procedure so that the child does not become an Estonian citizen. The procedure is exactly the same, the effect or the effect is reversed. When we talk about the substance of the problem, I do not understand why the IRL is so persistently trying to keep us in a situation where we are constantly restoring citizenshiplessness, which is opposed to all the positions of human rights organisations. A large number of them have also pointed out this, but the principle opposes it by all human rights organisations that have dealt with citizenship issues at all levels. Regeneration of nationality should not be an objective of any country. I do not know why you have such an objective, why you consistently pursue it.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U164	No further questions. I'm opening negotiations. Ken-Marti Vaher, please! I'll give you more time.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U165	Thank you very much, Mr President. Honourable Members of the Riigikogu! We have before us today at second reading a draft which, in the opinion of the IRL Group and in the opinion of many experts, basically changes Estonian citizenship policy. In my opinion, this'regeneration of citizenshiplessness', which sounded several times in the recent debate, is not happening in Estonia. Our citizenship policy so far has been based on very clear rules for quite many years. They have been understandable even for those who have been calling for the so-called 'zero option' for years. In my opinion, such unjustified games, which have unfortunately fallen into the pre-election period, do not really deserve candles. They are short-sighted and certainly do not serve long-term interests in our society. There are three important pillars in this draft that are going to change. The first pillar, which has also been discussed at length here today, is the abolition of the requirement to express the will of a person or his representative in respect of the naturalisation of citizenship in a particular target group. This has been one of the most important principles of our citizenship policy. The expression of wishes is required of people who wish to acquire Estonian citizenship by naturalisation. The change automatically means imposing Estonian citizenship, which has so far been unthinkable. This solution does not make sense; it does not value the loyalty relationship that should arise between the Estonian state and a particular person. At a later stage, a person must, or must, perceive that it is not just a privilege that is added, but also a clear contractual relationship with unambiguous obligations. Let me remind you, for years now, the Estonian state has shown considerable hospitality to the children born here by stateless parents. No matter what some so-called human rights organisations and their reports say, these children receive Estonian citizenship as a result of a certain procedure, but basically without any conditions. To do so, however, a clear declaration of the parent's will is expected. For years, the Estonian state has also made efforts to raise people's awareness and inform them of the right their child has when it comes to two stateless parents. In my opinion, the abolition of the requirement to express will, the automatic imposition of citizenship, also means the devaluation of citizenship as a valuable privilege, which also opens the door to further dilutions, which, unfortunately, have been discussed in this Chamber even today. Secondly, our citizenship policy so far has been based on the clear principle that, at the same time, a person can have one citizenship and a loyalty relationship with one country. This means refusing to have multiple nationality at the same time. In particular, this concerns those who acquire naturalisation. This bill, however, secretly legitimizes double citizenship, creates an advantage for minors who have acquired Estonian or other national citizenship by naturalisation, and essentially encourages parents to take Estonian and Russian citizenship for their children, for example. Having such double citizenship before the age of 21 is permitted in the future. In my opinion, there is no reasonable justification for creating such an advantage. There are a few cases where a parent has justified his or her child's taking two citizenships with knowledge of the fact that he or she did not know about the double citizenship ban, but this should not cancel the objective of treating everyone equally. Nor can it outweigh the fundamental principle that law-abiding people should not be put at a disadvantage than those who ignore the double citizenship ban. This amendment gives the narrow target group a clear advantage, which the absolute majority of the Estonian population do not have. And thirdly, the requirements of the language examination for people aged 65 and over are also diluted, the written part is waived. This is, in my opinion, a regrettable signal, which gives other potential applicants the false hope that the language requirements will be further reduced and will certainly offend those good citizens who have acquired citizenship by fulfilling the requirements of the written examination. The fact is also that people who have now reached the age of 65 and have not yet acquired any ability to express themselves in writing in Estonian were about twenty years younger, or golden middle age, in the golden forties when this requirement was introduced. It is quite clear that most of them do not have a written examination but a clear will to apply for citizenship, but the existence of will is also an extremely important principle when we talk about the acquisition of citizenship by naturalisation. Ultimately, in my opinion, such a game is not sensible in terms of citizenship policy. This is certainly not justified for a long time, nor is it a considered angle of approach. I also think that this foreign-language population, for whom this draft is primarily intended as a signal before the elections, actually places a great deal of importance in its life, which does not have the frameworks or limits of language or citizenship, it is better to cope, better jobs, better education or so on. Clear rules in Estonia have been a big plus in my opinion so far. The fundamentals of citizenship policy have persisted and there is no point in walking around with them lightly. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U166	Jaak Aaviksoo, please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U167	Good President! Ladies and gentlemen, Civil society is the basis for the survival of every state, including Estonia, and according to our constitution, the supreme authority of state. This is why citizenship issues are understandable at the centre of any political debate and should be taken extremely seriously. There must be a well-known need to amend the Citizenship Act. However, we heard a few moments ago from this very talking board that this change was largely reflected in external pressure I do not know what the initiators of this draft mean by human rights violations or racial discrimination in the Republic of Estonia. As far as I know, the right to citizenship is not regarded as a human right by any international organisation. But let it be the responsibility of the initiators of the bill. We have gradually naturalised a considerable number of loyal citizens, both from other nations and from stateless persons, with the support of the Citizenship Act based on clear principles so far. I dare confirm that Estonia's citizenship policy has been successful and is based in the best way on the interests of the Estonian people. Among other things, it should be noted positively that the large number of stateless persons acquired as a legacy of the Soviet occupation is decreasing in Estonia. However, we have so far not given in to the pressure to introduce the so-called 'zero option' of citizenship and citizenship at birth, which is contrary to national interests. We have been guided by the so-called 'blood-based citizenship' principle, which is applied by most countries. In addition to other censorships, the ability of the Estonian language plays an important role in obtaining naturalisation from the perspective of Estonian ethnic self-consciousness. And whatever is rhetorically claimed, this bill will change these foundations of citizenship policy. First, the bill violates the fundamental principle of blood-based citizenship and allows Estonian citizens to become born-based and without a declaration of will. One could take a step further: we impose citizenship on people without asking for their will or despite it. How it serves the national interests of Estonia is difficult for me to understand. Secondly, by abandoning the requirement of a written national language examination for some applicants, we give in to the forces that want the general relaxation or even the abolition of language requirements. Again, it is difficult for me to understand how it serves the national interests of Estonia. It is true that the Citizenship Act is in some respects too inflexible and does not allow for decision-making in justified cases, including for humane reasons. This situation should be changed so as not to exploit individual cases where discretion could indeed be exercised when deciding whether to grant citizenship in the interests of propaganda that are incomprehensible. I repeat, this situation should be changed, but its price must not be to abandon the principles. On behalf of the IRL Group, I propose that we interrupt the second reading of draft law 737 so that we can remain true to our principles, maintain the positive experience of the current citizenship policy and eliminate the shortcomings that we see together with a greater consensus than has been the case so far. Thank you!

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U206	The seventh item is the second reading of Bill 794 of the Tax Exchange Act initiated by the Government of the Republic. I ask Aivar Sõerdi, Member of the Monetary Commission, to present the speech chair.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U207	Mr President, I would like to thank you very much. Ladies and gentlemen, The Finance Committee discussed the draft Tax Exchange Act in preparation for its second reading at its meeting on 15 and 16 December. Once again, I will give a brief overview of the substance of the draft. Its content is the exchange of information in tax matters. National law is intended to bring into line with the Agreement on the exchange of information in tax matters concluded with the United States and ratified in June. Secondly, the aim is to create the prerequisites for implementing the new EU and OECD tax exchange standards. The purpose of the exchange of information is to reduce the possibilities for taxable persons in the United States of America to hide income and evade taxes. This concerns us because if the financial institution does not provide the requested information to the United States of America, 30% of the payments made by that financial institution will be withheld in the United States of America. In essence, it is a sanctioning rate. In order to bring national law into line with the FATCA Agreement, the same new law, the Tax Exchange Act, will be created. The integration of international rules on automatic tax exchange into the new law makes them understandable for taxpayers, financial institutions and the Tax and Customs Board supervising the whole matter. There is also a presumption that the new EU and OECD tax exchange standards based on FATCA will be transposed next year. The law would still need to be adopted this year so that all financial institutions can meet the new reporting period starting on 1 January on a uniform and clear basis. I would also stress that this law does not create any new tax liability or increase the tax liability of persons complying with tax laws. The FATCA Agreement and, more generally, any exchange of tax information are intended only to reduce cross-border tax evasion. No amendments came from the Riigikogu by the deadline of 12 December. However, the Commission made four amendments. The Commission meetings, which took place on 15 and 16 December, were mainly interested in two issues. First, how it is established in the performance of the contract that the customer is resident for tax purposes in the United States. The answer was that the bank must exercise due diligence, including requiring written confirmations from the client. They require the bank to identify potential US tax residents and to transmit information about them to the tax authorities. In case of doubt, enquiries can be made, including to the tax authorities on the basis of tax agreements. The second issue was the question of what is the common part of tax residence and e-residency. The answer to this question was that tax residence is internationally agreed and defined, while e-residency, according to current knowledge, is the concept of using Estonian ID and IT opportunities by citizens of another country. There's just a verbal coincidence, basically, these things don't overlap. As I said, the Commission has tabled four amendments. Amendments 1 and 2 have been made in cooperation with the Estonian Banking Association, the Ministry of Finance and the Ministry of Justice and concern that the persons on whom the data are collected and transmitted have the right to be informed. Amendment 3 brings the text of the draft more closely into line with the financial institutions' right to decide on the application of more favourable rules for a certain period of time and makes a reference clarification. Amendment 4 sets out the date of entry into force of the draft, which is 1 January 2015. I admit that the government initiated the draft a little late and did not take into account the normal pace of the procedure of the Riigikogu. At the same time, the Commission is convinced that a law is needed from the beginning of next year, and that has also been said by the market participants themselves, with whom we had the opportunity to meet. The consensual proposal of the Committee on Monetary Affairs is to conclude the second reading and, if the second reading is closed, to take the final vote on Thursday, i.e. tomorrow. This too was a consensual decision. Thank you for your attention.
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U208	Thank you. Any questions for the waitress? There are no questions. Thank you, Aivar Sõerd! Do you want to open negotiations? No calls, no negotiations. Let us examine the amendments. These have all been done by the commission, the Committee on Monetary Affairs, so there is a proposal to take them fully into account. So, Amendment 1, No 2, No 3, No 4. The amendments have been examined. The commission has proposed that the second reading of draft 794 be terminated. Second reading is over.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U212	Our ninth item on the agenda is the first reading of the draft law amending the Code of Enforcement Procedure initiated by the Government of the Republic and related amendments to other laws 803. I would like to present the speech chair to Minister of Justice Andres Anvelt!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U213	Madam President! Happy Members of the Riigikogu! I would like to introduce you to the draft law amending the law on enforcement proceedings initiated by the Government of the Republic and, in this connection, to the amendment of other laws, the so-called draft law on maintenance. With this draft, we are trying to take a major step towards ensuring that the money needed, first and foremost, for their children can be recovered from the evaders of the payment of a living. The aim of the draft is to provide for robust national enforcement measures for the evasion of child maintenance payments. The state must support children, but if parents fail to fulfil their obligations, some of them still live at risk of poverty. The draft includes changes that have a direct impact on people who owe a child's livelihood. The goal is to make people pay for their living more conscientiously. The nature of the problem is also illustrated by the fact that every year the courts make more than 1,000 decisions on how to make a living, some of which go into the hands of bailiffs because the parents do not pay for a living. The bailiffs are also unable to recover a very large amount of maintenance claims. As of the beginning of the year, for example, there are more than 12,000 maintenance claims in enforcement proceedings and the resulting debt of more than EUR 10 million. In order to improve this situation, we have provided leverage for the whole package, which should significantly improve the state of affairs. In the future, the court will be able to suspend the debtor's right to drive a motor vehicle, as well as a recreational craft, to withdraw his hunting certificate, weapons permit and fishing card in the event of his failure to pay without a valid reason for the maintenance. It is important to point out here that the suspension of these rights is decided by the court and the court will certainly consider whether or not the debtor of a child's livelihood needs a driving licence to work and earn a living. Certainly, the court will not suspend the validity of professional drivers' driving licences because they are needed to make money to pay for a living. From now on, the bailiff is obliged to contact the debtor every two months to find out why he or she is not fulfilling the child's maintenance obligation, the amount of his or her income and how he or she intends to liquidate the debt. In cases where it has not been possible to recover maintenance from the debtor, the bailiff shall, at least once a year, search the premises and land held by the debtor. This will give the bailiff a better overview of what the debtor really has. In the future, the debtor of a child's livelihood will not be able to receive support from a private forest owner and start-up support. The law includes the condition that the application for the allowance is rejected if the person is subject to enforcement proceedings in respect of the child's maintenance. The limitation period for a child's maintenance claim is extended from three to ten years, as the practice has shown that a maintenance claim cannot always be recovered within three years. An adult student in a higher education institution is entitled to claim a child's livelihood until he or she reaches the age of 21. Under current law, an adult youth has the right to claim maintenance from the parent until the age of 21 if he or she continues to complete primary or secondary education in primary, secondary or vocational schools. It must be acknowledged that there is no real reason to distinguish between these young people, both children who receive secondary education and children who receive university education need the same parental support. The bill clarifies the concept of the debtor's income to be seized and allows the income to be frozen by including the benefit in kind received in relation to the person's employment relationship. Some debtors are also granted benefits in kind by their employers, such as free use of a car or a place of residence. With the bill, the bailiff can count the benefits in kind from such an employment relationship as a person's remuneration. As a result, the debtor's considerable income increases and the bailiff is able to seize the cash payment to a large extent. In addition, bailiffs shall be given the right to seize the funds which the debtor is obliged to pay to third parties if the financial obligations paid to third parties in one month exceed the amount of the debtor's non-secured income provided for in one month. The amendment seeks to obtain hidden money from a bailiff paid to third parties for the performance of the debtor's obligations in cash or using other persons' accounts. When applying for state legal aid, children are exempted from explaining their financial position in the matter of maintenance. Removing this obligation will make it easier to apply for state legal aid in a maintenance case and will speed up the examination of applications. Finally, I note that the law is designed to enter into force in two parts. The provisions of the draft act concerning the restriction of the debtor's rights will enter into force on 1 March 2016 in order to allow sufficient time for the development of registers. The remaining provisions will enter into force on 1 July next year, leaving bailiffs a transitional period to adapt to the new regulation. I hope that the draft will receive great support in the Riigikogu! Thank you!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U214	Thank you very much. The question is Margus Tsahknal. Please!
ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U215	Thank you. Minister, thank you very much. I would first like to acknowledge that such a bill has reached this Chamber of the Riigikogu. ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U216	Thank you. First of all, thank you for the credit! Personally, I certainly support the idea of this livelihood fund, and I think that it is only with this attitude that it is possible to ensure equal treatment for all, say, children in different positions and their single parents. It is a fact that even with the ideal functioning of the future law being discussed today, some debtors may lose their child's livelihood.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U228	As I said in my report, the expiry date for a child's maintenance claim will be extended from three to ten years.

ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U231	Yes, I am largely of the same opinion. One goal is to force reckless parents to carry out their duties. ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U233	Thank you. In response to my good colleague Liisa, I understand what this bill and the livelihood fund are all about. ParlaMint-EE_2014-12-17_U245	President-in-Office of the Council. Ladies and gentlemen, As the debate did indeed turn out to be that the mash and cabbages came in, I would like to say, as an introduction, that in the committee session, the issue of this fund also came up well. I also state my personal position: this fund itself is not a means of escape or a miracle. In fact, the solution is that parents should freely agree with each other, honestly fulfilling their responsibilities. If they do not do so, this bill will be a very good step towards the country implementing the coercive and the pressure for parents to do so. The title of the draft is currently the 'Law amending the Code of Enforcement Procedure and related amendments to other laws'. It could be peacefully renamed the Law of Raven Elders. After all, this is a serious step towards helping parents who find it difficult to get a living from the other side. But now I will go a little bit more directly to the Commission's debate. This draft was discussed by the Commission on Monday, 8 December. Igor Gräzin, Jüri Morozov, Kalle Jents, Kalle Laane, Mikhail Korb and Valdo Randpere, and Linnar Liivamägi and Raini Laid, took part. There was no Ken-Mart Vaher, Marko Pomerants and Peter Võsa. We were presented by Andres Anvelt, Minister of Justice, and by Kristen Kanarik, Minister of Justice. When discussing the draft, the Commission raised some questions. For example, the age limit for the payment of maintenance allowance rose, which this bill intends to change. At present, parents are obliged to pay a living for their children until they reach the age of 21 when they study in comprehensive school or upper secondary school. This bill also introduces studying at a higher education institution: it also gives the child the right to ask for a living. We also touched on the question of how we can identify the incomes that debtors are trying to hide. We were briefed on the rights and obligations of the executive officer to carry out searches against the debtor. Once again, on the other side, I would like to stress what the Minister said in this Chamber: the problem is that, if we compare the amount of money owed, this amount has increased tenfold from 2006 until this year. This is not only about EUR 10 million, but also about the fact that the amount has increased tenfold. The trend is very worrying, and that is why the Ministry of Justice is in front of us with such a bill. But what, in turn, have they relied on? They haven't invented it in a completely empty place. From the point of view of Estonian legislation, it is a "milestone" in foreign language, a kilometer post in Estonian. Similar practices are applied in quite a number of foreign countries, including the United States, Canada, the Czech Republic and Slovakia. By the way, in Australia, debtors of a child's livelihood are forbidden to leave the country. Looks like it's very effective. The findings of the studies are known specifically from the United States of America. Some states have put in place a procedure that allows the debtor of a child's livelihood to be temporarily deprived of the right to drive a motor vehicle, to withdraw professional and professional licences, and to withdraw recreational and sporting licences. The State of Washington compared the effectiveness of maintenance requirements six months before the suspension of the right to drive and six months after the implementation of this measure. The survey showed that three times more money could be recovered after the suspension of the right to drive. That could be a good tip for us. These measures are effective and maybe we don't have to think too much about this dream fund. This fund also means, unfortunately, that honest taxpayers will assume the duties of raven parents. Basically, that should not be the case. Based on my experience, I can tell all parents that, of course, this is a bitter moment when there is a divorce and you have to start paying for a living. But when you have gone through this period, believe me, you will find that you have paid too little, and you will be proud that you have never erred against this obligation. So pay your children a living, and don't get any shit out of it! That's what I want to say to everyone. At the same time, it is good to note that if Estonia goes this way, that the court decides to implement these measures, in certain cases it is still possible not to let them go. This is when the debtor identifies very compelling reasons why they should not be applied to him. As the Minister has already pointed out, the suspension of a professional driver's licence may deprive him of his ability to pay a living allowance. At the same time, there was a discussion in the Commission that, on the one hand, this sanction really would prevent him from paying a living allowance, but in fact, he has come into this situation precisely because he has not yet paid the child's money. There's a bit of a contradiction around here somewhere. The guy's got a driver's license, but he hasn't been volunteering without money, he's probably just hiding his income. It will be quite difficult to prove income in this situation. There's gonna be some kind of interruption somewhere. For example, the debtor can justify not paying by not having a job, being unemployed. Current practice shows that around 40% of those against whom a claim for maintenance is made do not pay, but only 4.5% of them have registered as unemployed. All I can say on behalf of the Commission is that I invite all the raven elders to come to their senses. You're not talking about taking money from you for some abstract purpose completely meaningless. This is money for your children, and if you wanted and were able to acquire these children in this world, you can take care of them too! That concludes my report.
